# Yantra language — core specification v1

Status: **normative.** Where this file and any other doc disagree, this file wins; where it is
silent, the behaviour is undefined and must be decided here (QUESTIONS.md) before anyone
implements it. Every rule has an id `[S-…]`; every id must be covered by at least one test in
`sdk/tests/spec/` whose name contains the id (enforced by `docs/spec/coverage` — PLAN L10).

Yantra is Nim plus a standard library of macros. Nothing here changes Nim's own semantics; it
defines what the Yantra macros accept and what they mean.

---

## 1. Syntax shapes

**[S-SYN-1] Two call shapes, one meaning.** Every Yantra construct can be written as a command
(statement position) or a call (any position). They are identical:

```nim
text "Hello", size = 32            # command — statement position only
let t = text("Hello", size = 32)   # call — required in expression position
```

**[S-SYN-2] Expression position needs parentheses.** `let x = text "a", size = 1` is a Nim
syntax error. Style rule (and `yantra fmt`): commands for statements, calls for expressions.

**[S-SYN-3] Argument order.** `kind(main, named…)` then an optional `:` block of children.
The *main* argument is positional and first (`text` → content, `image` → source, `button` →
label, `input` → bound value). All other props are named. A second positional argument is a
compile error `Y010 positional after main`.

**[S-SYN-4] Children.** A `:` block after a container holds its children, in order. Any Nim
statement may appear in a children block (`let`, `var`, `if`, `for`, `case`, proc calls);
only statements that *create nodes* add children.

**[S-SYN-5] Events are blocks.** `onClick: stmts` or `onClick(e): stmts` (with the event
value). An event block is a closure; it may read and write any visible variable.

**[S-SYN-6] Identifiers are Nim identifiers.** Nim ignores case and underscores after the first
letter: `fontSize`, `font_size` and `fontsize` are the same name, and **`eNum` is the keyword
`enum`**. Yantra style is camelCase; the formatter normalises. A prop or node name that
collides with a Nim keyword under this rule is forbidden in the library.

**[S-SYN-7] Reserved names.** Inside UI blocks the names of node kinds, props and events are
resolved by the Yantra macros first, then by normal Nim scoping. A local variable that shadows a
node kind inside a UI block is a compile error `Y011 shadows node kind 'text'` — rename it.

**[S-SYN-8] Naming a node (PROPOSED — decided by the Phase I A/B test).** A lowercase label
before a node statement names it, so timelines and handlers can refer to it without `let`:

```nim
film "Launch", format = vertical1080:
  shot "hook", 3.s:
    title: words "Apps without the bloat", size = 140
    hero: column(gap = 2):
      tag: text "60 KB apps", size = 48
    timeline:
      title.reveal(stagger = 60.ms)
      tag.opacity.to(1, 400.ms)
```

Why: it is a statement, so the command-in-expression trap ([S-SYN-2]) cannot happen; the name
comes first, which reads well and gives stable handles for targeted AI edits. A label is a
lowercase identifier not equal to any event name; PascalCase names are components. Candidates
being measured against it: `let x = text(...)` and `text "…", id = x`.

**[S-SYN-9] Nim keywords are never names in the library.** `out`, `export`, `from`, `type`,
`method`, `iterator`, `object`, `static`, `bind`, `mixin`, `using`, `when`, `of`, `in`, `is`, `as`,
`div`, `mod`, `shl`, `shr`, `and`, `or`, `not`, `xor`, `enum`, `tuple`, `concept`, `distinct`,
`block`, `defer`, `yield`, `addr`, `ptr`, `ref`, `var`, `let`, `const` (and every other Nim keyword,
compared style-insensitively per [S-SYN-6]) must not be node kinds, props, events, verbs or
recommended labels. The library uses `save` (not `export`), `final`/`result` (not `out`),
`source` (not `from`). A test in `sdk/tests/spec/` checks every generated name against the Nim
keyword list. Authors who use a keyword as a label get `Y012 'out' is a Nim keyword — try 'final'`.

**[S-SYN-10] No expressiveness ceiling.** Every construct in the standard library is written in
the same public language using lower levels (architecture §7.2): verbs → nodes → scene API → draw →
GPU shaders/compute → plain Nim → native plugins. Any computation a VFX tool, game engine or
browser can perform must be expressible in a Yantra program; the north stars (`docs/northstar.md`)
are the executable proof and may never be removed or simplified to make them pass.

**[S-SYN-11] The Yantra prelude is imported implicitly.** App modules do not require `import yantra`;
the full Yantra prelude (`sdk/yantra.nim`, including DSL verbs, signals, components, and node kinds)
is imported implicitly by the compiler, `config.nims`, and build tooling. Explicit `import yantra`
remains valid and produces no duplicate import warnings or errors.

## 2. Nodes

**[S-NODE-1]** A node is created by evaluating a node-kind call inside a UI block (`app`,
`component`, `scene`, `doc`, `site`, `deck`, `film`, `game`). Node-kind calls outside a UI block
are compile errors `Y020 node outside UI block`.

**[S-NODE-2] Identity.** A node's identity is its position in the *static* structure of the
source plus, inside `for`, its key (§4). Identity decides what survives a re-render: state,
focus, scroll position, running animations.

**[S-NODE-3]** A node has exactly one parent. Moving a node = remove + insert; its identity (and
therefore its state) is preserved only when moved by a keyed `for`.

**[S-NODE-4] Accessibility.** Every node kind declares a role (`protocol/schema.nim`); a node
kind without a role does not compile (Phase Y). Text content is the default accessible name;
`label = "…"` overrides it.

## 3. Props

**[S-PROP-1] Declared, typed, closed.** Each node kind has a declared prop set. Unknown prop →
compile error `Y001 unknown prop 'colour' on text — did you mean 'color'?` (edit distance ≤ 2).

**[S-PROP-2] Resolution order** (highest wins): explicit value at the call site → value passed
by the enclosing component → the node kind's theme style → the kind's built-in default.

**[S-PROP-3] Reactive or static is inferred.** If a prop's value expression reads a signal or
derived value, the prop re-evaluates when those change; otherwise it is evaluated once at
creation. The author never marks this.

**[S-PROP-4] Types and conversions.** `int` → `float` is implicit; `float` → `int` is a compile
error. Lengths accept a bare number (= logical px, [S-PLAT-1]) or a unit (`16.px`, `50.pct`, `1.fr`, `2.em`).
Colours accept a theme token (`accent`, `fg.muted`) or `rgb"#RRGGBB"` / `rgba"#RRGGBBAA"`. See [S-COL-1..4] for precision.
Durations must carry a unit (`300.ms`, `2.s`); a bare number is `Y002 duration needs a unit`.
Angles: `90.deg`, `1.5.rad`.

**[S-PROP-5] Spacing and size scales.** `pad`, `gap`, `margin` with a bare int `n` mean theme
step `n` (step 0 = 0, 1 = 4 px, 2 = 8, 3 = 12, 4 = 16, 5 = 24, 6 = 32, 7 = 48, 8 = 64);
an explicit unit (`pad = 10.px`) bypasses the scale. `size` on text accepts a scale name
(`xs sm md lg xl display`) or a number (px).

**[S-PROP-6] Out-of-range values** are clamped and reported once as a warning in debug builds:
`opacity` → [0, 1]; negative `width/height/pad/gap/radius` → 0; `fontSize` ≤ 0 → default.
NaN or ±Inf in any float prop → runtime error `PropValueError` (never silently drawn).

**[S-PROP-7] Setting the same prop twice** at one call site is `Y003 duplicate prop`.

## 4. Control flow in UI blocks

**[S-CTL-1] `if / elif / else` and `case`** are reactive when their conditions read signals.
Switching branches disposes the old branch's nodes, effects and resources (§5.6) and creates the
new branch's. Branches with no nodes are allowed.

**[S-CTL-2] `for x in xs`** is reactive when `xs` is a signal or derived value. Items are keyed:
- default key = the item itself when the item type is a value type with `==` and `hash`
  (ints, strings, value objects); otherwise the index;
- explicit key: `for t in todos, key = t.id:` (the expression is evaluated per item).
Keyed items keep their identity when reordered; unkeyed (index) items keep identity by position.

**[S-CTL-3] Duplicate keys** in one `for` → runtime error `DuplicateKey(key, positions)` in debug
builds; in release builds the later duplicate is keyed by `(key, occurrence)`.

**[S-CTL-4]** `while`, `break`, `continue`, `return` inside a UI block follow Nim rules but are not
reactive (evaluated once at creation). A `for` over a non-reactive collection is also evaluated
once.

**[S-CTL-5] Empty collections** render nothing; use `else` on `for` for an empty state:
`for t in todos: … else: text "Nothing yet"` (Yantra extension; compiles to an `if`).

## 5. Reactivity

Values: `state` (writable signal), `derived` (computed, read-only), `effect` (side effect).

```nim
var count = state 0                         # signal
let doubled = derived: count * 2            # lazy, memoised
effect: log "count is {count}"              # runs after the tree commits
```

**[S-RX-1] Reading** a signal inside a tracking scope (prop expression, `derived`, `effect`,
reactive `if/for` condition) subscribes that scope. Reading outside a tracking scope (e.g. in an
event handler) just returns the current value and subscribes nothing. `untrack: expr` reads
without subscribing inside a tracking scope.

**[S-RX-2] Writing** (`count += 1`, `count.set v`) is synchronous for reads: the next line sees
the new value. Propagation to the tree is **batched**: all writes in one event handler, one
frame callback, or one `batch:` block propagate once, at the end.

**[S-RX-3] Equality cut-off.** A write that is `==` to the current value does nothing. For
`seq`/`Table`/objects, mutate in place with `count.update(proc(v: var T) = …)`; `update` always
notifies. Direct mutation of a signal's inner value without `update` is a compile error for
container types (`Y030 mutate through update`).

**[S-RX-4] Glitch-free.** Within one propagation, every derived value and effect observes a
consistent snapshot; each runs at most once, in topological order. No derived value ever sees a
half-updated state.

**[S-RX-5] Derived values are lazy and memoised**: computed on first read, recomputed only when a
dependency changed and someone reads them again. Dependencies are dynamic (re-tracked each run),
so `derived: (if flag: a else: b)` depends only on the branch taken.

**[S-RX-6] Forbidden inside `derived`**: writing any signal (`Y031` at compile time when
visible; `WriteInDerived` at run time otherwise) and I/O. `derived` bodies are `func`-pure
where the compiler can prove it.

**[S-RX-7] Cycles.** A derived value that (transitively) reads itself → runtime error
`ReactiveCycle(path)` naming every node in the cycle. An effect that writes a signal it reads is
allowed but re-runs at most **100** times per propagation; beyond that → `ReactiveOverflow(path)`.

**[S-RX-8] Effects** run after the tree for the current propagation is committed, in creation
order. An effect may register cleanup with `onCleanup: …`, run before its next execution and on
disposal.

**[S-RX-9] Ownership and disposal.** Every signal, derived, effect, resource, timeline and event
subscription is owned by the node scope that created it. When that scope is disposed (branch
switch, keyed item removed, component unmounted, app closed), everything it owns is disposed in
reverse creation order, cleanups run, in-flight resources are cancelled. Nothing leaks by design.

**[S-RX-10] Signals in closures.** Event handlers and async procs capture signals by reference;
they always see the current value. Capturing a *plain* `var` in a handler captures the Nim
variable as usual (not reactive).

## 6. Components

```nim
proc Card(title: string, count: Val[int] = 0, onOpen: Handler = nil) {.component.} =
  box(pad = 4, radius = 12):
    text "{title} ({count})"
    button "Open":
      onClick:
        if onOpen != nil: onOpen()
    slot()
```

**[S-CMP-1]** A component is an ordinary Nim `proc` with the `{.component.}` pragma — the only
form (one way to say it, [S-TOK-1]). Parameters are ordinary typed parameters with defaults;
editors and nimsuggest understand it as a normal proc. Components are named in PascalCase. Recursion is allowed (tree views); runtime depth > 256 → `ComponentDepth` error.

**[S-CMP-2] `Val[T]`** accepts a plain `T`, a `state T` or a `derived T`. Inside the component,
reading a `Val[T]` is uniform and reactive when the caller passed a reactive value.

**[S-CMP-3] Two-way binding** only through an explicit `Bind[T]` parameter (the caller passes a
`state`): `input value` inside `component Field(value: Bind[string])`.

**[S-CMP-4] Slots.** `slot()` renders the caller's children block; named slots:
`slot(header)` with caller syntax `header: …`. Calling a component with children when it has no
`slot()` → `Y040 component has no slot`.

**[S-CMP-5]** Component-local `state` is created per instance and preserved as long as the
instance's identity (§2) survives.

## 7. Events

**[S-EVT-1]** Handlers run synchronously on the host's UI turn, inside a batch (§S-RX-2).
Default actions (focus, text insertion, scroll) happen after the handler unless it calls
`e.preventDefault()`.

**[S-EVT-2] Propagation.** Pointer and key events go target → ancestors (bubble); `e.stop()`
stops it. No capture phase in v1.

**[S-EVT-3] Exceptions in handlers** never crash the host: they propagate to the nearest
`errorBoundary(fallback):` ancestor; with none, the app shows the built-in error panel and the
exception is logged with the source location. The tree state before the handler is kept
(writes made before the raise are kept; propagation of them still happens).

**[S-EVT-4]** A handler that runs longer than 50 ms logs a slow-handler warning in debug builds
(apps are single-threaded; long work belongs in `spawn`/async).

## 8. Async and resources

**[S-ASY-1]** `fetch(...)`, `store` queries and other services return `Resource[T]` with
`loading`, `failed`, `error`, `value`, and `refresh()`. Reading `value` while `loading` or
`failed` raises `ResourceNotReady` — branch on the state first.

**[S-ASY-2] Latest wins.** When a reactive input of a resource changes, the in-flight request is
cancelled and a new one starts; a stale response is never delivered.

**[S-ASY-3]** `proc x() {.async.}` + `await` for sequential flows. Awaiting in a disposed scope
cancels the proc with `Cancelled` (no code after the `await` runs).

**[S-ASY-4]** Timeouts are explicit (`timeout = 10.s`, default 30 s for network); expiry sets
`failed` with `Timeout`.

## 9. Time and motion

**[S-TIME-1] Timeline composition.** Statements in `timeline:` run in sequence; duration of a
sequence = sum. `all:` runs children in parallel; duration = max. `wait d` adds `d`.
`loop n:` repeats its body `n` times (`n ≥ 1`); `loop:` (no count) is only allowed in apps, not
in `film`/`scene` (a video must have a finite length) → `Y050`.

**[S-TIME-2] `x.to(v, d, ease)`** tweens from the value `x` has *at the tween's start time* in
the timeline (resolved deterministically from earlier tracks, not from wall-clock state).

**[S-TIME-3] Sampling.** `sample(t)` for `t < 0` → start values; for `t ≥ duration` → end values;
at an exact boundary between two sequential tweens the later tween's start value is used.
Zero-duration tweens jump. Negative durations → `Y051` (literal) or `TimelineError` (computed).

**[S-TIME-4] Easing** functions are defined exactly (formulas in `sdk/motion/easing.nim`, matching
the standard Penner/CSS definitions) and must satisfy `e(0) == 0`, `e(1) == 1`.

**[S-TIME-5] Conflicts in apps.** A new `to` on a prop that is already animating cancels the
running tween and starts from the prop's current displayed value (“overwrite auto”).

**[S-TIME-6] Frames.** Films/scenes render at their declared fps; frame `n` is `t = n / fps`
exactly (rational time, no float drift). Apps animate on the display's refresh; `onFrame(dt)`
receives the real elapsed time clamped to ≤ 100 ms.

## 10. Text

**[S-TXT-1] Interpolation.** In string literals inside UI blocks, `{expr}` inserts any Nim
expression converted with `$`; `{expr:fmt}` uses strformat specs (`{price:.2f}`); `{{` and `}}`
are literal braces. The literal is reactive when any `expr` reads a signal.

**[S-TXT-2]** Strings from variables (not literals) are **not** interpolated — braces in user
data are shown as typed. (No template injection.)

**[S-TXT-3]** All text is UTF-8; lengths, cursor positions and selections count grapheme
clusters, never bytes (Indic conjuncts and emoji are single units to the user).

**[S-TXT-4]** Number/date formatting uses the app locale (`en-IN` → 12,34,567.00 lakh grouping);
`{n:raw}` opts out.

## 11. Errors

**[S-ERR-1] Compile-time errors** have stable codes `Yxxx`, point at the user's source line (not
generated code), and include a fix suggestion where one exists. The list lives in
`sdk/errors.nim`; every code has a test that triggers it.

**[S-ERR-2] Runtime errors** are typed exceptions (`PropValueError`, `DuplicateKey`,
`ReactiveCycle`, `ReactiveOverflow`, `ResourceNotReady`, `Cancelled`, `Timeout`,
`CapabilityDenied`, `TimelineError`, `ComponentDepth`) carrying structured fields.

**[S-ERR-3]** An app never crashes the host. A trap inside the wasm module (out-of-bounds, stack
overflow, unreachable) stops that app, shows the error panel, and keeps the host alive.

## 12. Determinism

**[S-DET-1]** Given the same source, inputs and timestamps, `film`/`scene` output is identical
frame for frame on every machine. `rand` is seeded (default seed = hash of the file name);
wall-clock, network and unseeded randomness are unavailable inside `film`/`scene` → `Y060`.

**[S-DET-2]** Iteration order of anything that affects output is defined (no hash-order
iteration); `Table` iteration in UI blocks uses insertion order (`OrderedTable` semantics).

## 14. Platforms (desktop, web, phone, tablet, VR)

**[S-PLAT-1] Logical pixels.** `px` and bare lengths are *logical* pixels (1 px = 1/96 inch at
100 % scale, like CSS px). The platform multiplies by the device scale; apps never see physical
pixels unless they ask (`screen.scale`). In VR, 2D panels map logical px to metres via the
panel's size; 3D lengths use metres (`1.2.m`, `30.cm`).

**[S-PLAT-2] One pointer model.** Mouse, touch, pen, controller ray and hand pinch all produce
the same events: `onPointerDown/Move/Up/Cancel`, `onClick` (= tap), `onHover` (never fires for
touch). `e.device` tells which (`mouse, touch, pen, ray, hand`); `e.pointerId` distinguishes
simultaneous touches. Gestures are recognisers on top: `onTap, onDoubleTap, onLongPress,
onPan, onPinch, onRotate, onSwipe`. An app written only against `onClick` works on every device.
Hover-only functionality is a lint warning (touch and VR users could not reach it).

**[S-PLAT-3] Environment is reactive.** Read-only signals: `screen.width/height` (logical px),
`screen.scale`, `screen.orientation`, `env.safeArea` (insets), `env.keyboardInset`,
`env.darkMode`, `env.reducedMotion`, `env.textScale`, `env.device` (`desktop, phone, tablet,
headset, web`). Layout that depends on them updates automatically.
`if screen.width < 600: …` is the responsive pattern; `site` breakpoints compile to this.

**[S-PLAT-4] Lifecycle.** `onSuspend:` (save work; ≤ 1 s budget), `onResume:`, `onLowMemory:`.
The platform snapshots `state` values before suspend and restores them after a cold restart,
so an app killed in the background comes back where it was.

**[S-PLAT-5] Respecting the user.** With `env.reducedMotion`, timelines in apps (not films) run
at duration 0 unless marked `essential = true`. Text honours `env.textScale` (layout must not
clip at 200 %). Touch targets are at least 44 × 44 logical px (lint warning below).

**[S-PLAT-6] Same source everywhere.** Platform-specific code is allowed only through
`when target == android:` (compile-time) or `if env.device == headset:` (run-time); a capability
missing on a platform raises `CapabilityDenied(reason = unsupported)`, never crashes.

## 15. Colour and pixel precision

**[S-COL-1] Colours are linear floating point internally.** A colour value is four `float32`
components (RGB + alpha) in **linear** light plus a **colour space** tag. `rgb"#RRGGBB"` and theme
tokens are convenience inputs in sRGB and are converted on entry. 8-bit integers never appear
inside the pipeline; they exist only at the final display or 8-bit export step.

**[S-COL-2] Colour spaces are explicit.** Supported tags start with `srgb`, `linearSrgb`,
`displayP3`, `rec2020`, `acesCg`; conversion is done by OpenColorIO. Mixing two colours in different
spaces converts to the working space first (default `linearSrgb` for apps, `acesCg` for films).

**[S-COL-3] HDR is allowed.** Components may exceed 1.0 (bright light, glows). Clamping or tone
mapping happens only at output, by an explicit `toneMap` setting on the film/app (default: none for
UI, ACES filmic for films). NaN/Inf follow [S-PROP-6] (error, never drawn).

**[S-COL-4] Frame precision.** Films render into 16-bit float frames by default (32-bit on request);
export chooses the file depth (8-bit MP4, 10-bit HEVC/ProRes, 16/32-bit EXR). The same film gives
identical pixels at the same depth ([S-DET-1]).

## 13. Token economy (rules for the library, not the author)

**[S-TOK-1]** One way to say each thing; no aliases in the library.
**[S-TOK-2]** Every prop has a good default; a construct's minimal form is one line.
**[S-TOK-3]** No imports for the standard library (prelude); no boilerplate entry points.
**[S-TOK-4]** Names are full words, ≤ 12 characters where possible, no abbreviations
(`padding` → `pad` is the only exception family: `pad`, `gap`, `min`, `max`).
**[S-TOK-5]** The one-page API (`docs/llm.md`, generated) stays under 2 000 words.


================================================================
FILE: docs/apis.md
================================================================
# Platform APIs — fetch, storage, files and friends

Status: **proposal for Owner review.** How an app talks to the outside world.

## 1. How every API works (one pattern)

An app is a sandboxed wasm module: it cannot open a socket or a file. Every API is:

```
app (Nim, wasm)          protocol message            host service (native)
fetch(url)        ──►   {op: net.request, id: 7}  ──►  capability check ──► libcurl
reactive value    ◄──   {op: net.done, id: 7, …}  ◄──  worker thread finishes
```

- **Declared, deny-by-default.** The app's manifest lists what it needs; the user approves.
  An undeclared call is a typed error, never a silent success.
- **Asynchronous, never blocking.** Results arrive as **reactive values** (they plug straight
  into the UI and re-render only what uses them) or via `await` for step-by-step code.
- **Typed.** JSON is decoded into your Nim types by code generated at compile time (macros),
  so a missing field is a clear error, not `undefined`.
- **Same API everywhere.** Native host uses libcurl/SQLite; the web target (architecture §9)
  uses the browser's fetch/IndexedDB. App code does not change.
- Each service is a host plugin (architecture §7.3). Adding an API = one service + generated
  bindings; the SDK side never special-cases it.

## 2. Fetch

```nim
type Weather = object
  temp: float
  summary: string

app "Weather":
  needs network("api.example.com")                 # manifest; user approves once

  var city = state "Chennai"
  let w = fetch("https://api.example.com/v1/{city}").json(Weather)   # reactive resource

  column(gap = 2, pad = 4):
    input city
    if w.loading: spinner()
    elif w.failed: text "Couldn't load: {w.error.message}", color = danger
    else: text "{w.value.temp}° — {w.value.summary}", size = xl
```

- Changing `city` re-fetches automatically (like SWR / Solid resources); stale requests are
  cancelled.
- Options: `method`, `headers`, `body = json(obj)`, `timeout`, `retry`, `cache = 5.min`,
  `offline = true` (serve from store when offline).
- Step-by-step form when order matters:

```nim
proc checkout() {.async.} =
  let order = await post("https://api.shop.in/orders", json(cart)).json(Order)
  await payments.checkout(order.id, amount = order.total)   # platform capability
```

## 3. The standard services (all follow §1)

| API | Needs | Backed by |
|---|---|---|
| `fetch`, `post`, `websocket` | `network(domains)` | libcurl, libdatachannel |
| `store` (typed tables, key-value, sync) | `storage` (private per app, on by default) | SQLite |
| `files.open()` / `files.save()` | none — the user picks in a system dialog | host portal |
| `clipboard`, `notify`, `share` | `clipboard`, `notifications` | SDL3 / OS |
| `camera`, `mic`, `screen` | `camera`, `mic`, `screen` | SDL3, FFmpeg |
| `payments.checkout` | `payments` | trusted adapter (UPI/Razorpay) — app never sees credentials |
| `ai.chat`, `ai.image`, `ai.voice` | `ai` | llama.cpp / providers (`docs/ai.md`) |
| `auth.signIn` | `identity` | platform account; app gets a scoped token |
| `realtime.join(room)` | `network` | Yrs CRDT sync (architecture §10) |

`store` example:

```nim
type Note = object
  id: Id
  title, body: string

let notes = store.table(Note)                    # created on first use, schema from the type
notes.add Note(title: "Idea", body: "…")
for n in notes.where(title.contains "Idea"): …   # reactive query: UI updates on change
```

## 4. Tests

Every service ships a **fake** used in tests (`fetch` answers from recorded fixtures,
`payments` in sandbox mode), so apps are testable offline and deterministic.


================================================================
FILE: skills/yantra/SKILL.md
================================================================
---
name: yantra
description: Build native desktop apps and clone web pages pixel-for-pixel in Yantra, a Nim→WebAssembly UI framework rendered by a native SDL3 + Skia host. Use when asked to write a Yantra app, port an HTML file to Yantra, clone/replicate a website or app in Yantra, draw with a 2D canvas, build a 3D scene, synthesise audio, or find out what the Yantra renderer can and cannot draw. Every API here is read from the SDK source; nothing is from memory.
---

# Yantra

## What Yantra is, in five lines

1. A Yantra **app is one `.nim` file** that does `import yantra` and compiles to a small sandboxed WebAssembly module.
2. A **native host** — SDL3 + Skia + Yoga + wasmtime — loads that module, owns the node tree, dispatches events, and draws real native pixels.
3. The app has **no DOM, no CSS engine, no filesystem and no network**. It describes a UI by calling the host across a scalar wire protocol; that is the whole security model.
4. You describe a **node tree** (window → column → row → panel → text/button/canvas/…) and set **props** on it; **Yoga** does flex/grid layout and **Skia** does the drawing.
5. Anything procedural — a brush stroke, a chart, a molecule, a painting — goes in a **canvas** and you draw it yourself; anything structural goes in the node tree.

## Build and run — the exact commands

```bash
# BUILD (measured: ~9 s, exit 0)
bash tools/build-wasm.sh <src.nim> <out.wasm>              # debug
bash tools/build-wasm.sh <src.nim> <out.wasm> --release    # optimised + wasm-opt -Oz
```

Flags: `--release`, `--opt`, `--no-libc` (pure-computation module, no mini-libc).

Run it natively, and screenshot the first frame to check it renders:

```powershell
# a real window, with pointer + keyboard input
render/native/build/desktop/yantra_run.exe <out.wasm>

# headless: render frames through the REAL pointer pipeline and dump pixels
#   drive app.wasm out.raw W H SCALE SCRIPT
#   SCRIPT is SEMICOLON-separated: click:X,Y  drag:X1,Y1,X2,Y2  move:X,Y  key:N  text:S  wait:N
& .\render\native\build\drive.exe .\app.wasm .\out.raw 1280 800 1 "wait:45"
```

**The dump is BGRA, and you must swap it before any diff.**
`render/native/drive.nim:191-193` `copyMem`s a `seq[uint32]` of Skia
`0xAARRGGBB` pixels to disk, so the bytes on disk are **B,G,R,A**, while
`tools/pngdiff.mjs` reads bytes 0,1,2 as R,G,B. Always:

```bash
node tools/rawswap.mjs out.raw out_sw.raw     # once, never twice
node tools/pngdiff.mjs ref.png out_sw.raw 1280 800
```

Measured on one port, same reference, the swap being the only difference:
**91.45% unswapped vs 72.83% swapped.** The unswapped number looks plausible
and is wrong.

Full verification recipes: **`reference/01-build-run-and-verify.md`**.
Pixel parity: **`reference/21-visual-parity-verification.md`**.

## The program shape — a complete, verified, compiling app

This is `examples/hello_app.nim`. It builds clean. It is the whole language: a window, a tree, state, an event, and text that recomputes itself.

```nim
import yantra

app "Hello", width = 420, height = 260, fill = surface, pad = 24:
  var count = state(0)                       # reactive state

  column(spacing = 12, grow = 1):
    text "Clicks: {count}", fontSize = 28, font = mono

    if count > 0:
      text "{count} is a lot", fontSize = 14, fill = muted

    row(spacing = 8):
      button "Click me":
        count += 1
      button "Reset":
        count.set(0)
```

**Read this before you write anything:**

| You want to… | Do this |
|---|---|
| Declare a UI tree, bind state, react to events | the block DSL above — `app "T", width = …:` then an indented block |
| Reach the node itself (measure, animate, imperative handler) | the imperative style — `examples/imperative_app.nim` |
| Draw shapes, paths, text, images into a bitmap | a **`canvas`** node + the `Draw` builder |
| Accumulate strokes / animate frames | **`newLayer`** + `layer.draw(...)`, or `onFrame` |
| Reuse a chunk of UI | a `proc` marked `{.component.}` + `slot()` |
| Style many nodes the same | `style(fill = …, radius = 12)` applied with `use = …` |
| Repeat over data | `for x in items.keyed(x.id):` (+ `lazy` for long lists) |
| Toggle UI on a condition | `if` / `case` directly in the block |

## Where everything is written down

Everything an agent needs is in this folder. Read the chapter, not the guess.
**`reference/00-INDEX.md` is the full routed table** — it has one honest line per
chapter: what it is for, and what you will be able to do afterwards.

| Chapter | Read it when |
|---|---|
| `reference/01-build-run-and-verify.md` | building, running, screenshotting, the test gate |
| `reference/02-language-core.md` | the DSL: `app()`, tree, state, events, control flow |
| `reference/03-node-and-prop-reference.md` | **the lookup table** — every node kind and every prop |
| `reference/05-layout-and-containers.md` | flex, grid, sizing, padding, gap, alignment, sticky, scroll |
| `reference/06-text-and-fonts.md` | type, weights, families, **and where the `y` you pass actually lands** |
| `reference/07-color-gradients-and-effects.md` | colour model, themes, gradients, shadows, blend, opacity |
| `reference/08-images-icons-and-svg.md` | images, `fit`, SVG paths, the icon set |
| `reference/09-canvas-and-draw.md` | the 2D drawing API, layers, and its real limits |
| `reference/10-animation-and-motion.md` | `animate`, `transition`, easings, the frame loop |
| `reference/11-input-pointer-and-keys.md` | pointer, keyboard, focus, drag & drop |
| `reference/12-3d-and-shaders.md` | scene3d, models, instancing, custom shaders, particles |
| `reference/13-audio-video-and-data.md` | synth, WAV assets, video, storage, networking |
| `reference/15a-html-css-mapping.md` | **porting** — every CSS property and DOM/JS API → Yantra |

| `reference/20-cloning-a-live-website.md` | cloning a site that is not a local HTML file |
| `reference/21-visual-parity-verification.md` | proving a port matches its source, with a number |
| `reference/22-nim-closure-capture.md` | the closure-capture error that hits every drawing-heavy port |
| `reference/23-wasm-import-gate.md` | the app that loads as an empty tree because the host refused an import |
| `reference/24-draw-alpha-ramps.md` | vignettes, glows and fog — how to build a soft alpha ramp |
| `examples/` | two complete apps, both compile; copy from here |
| `translated-skills/` | the design/canvas/testing skills, translated to Yantra |
| `../web-to-yantra/SKILL.md` | **the second skill** — command-by-command HTML/CSS/Canvas2D/SVG/events → Yantra. Read this early for any port. |

There is **no chapter `04` and no `14`/`15`** — the numbering skips them. Do not
go looking; the gaps are not mistakes. There are 22 chapters plus this index.

## The four facts that will bite you if you do not know them

These are the ones that cost real rounds. Each is carried in full, with its
evidence, in the chapter named.

1. **`Draw.text(x, y, …)`'s `y` is the top of the paragraph line box, not the
   baseline.** `libs/nimskia/src/skia_shim.cpp:291` calls
   `p->paint(canvas, x, y)`. The doc comment at `libs/nimskia/src/nimskia.nim:76`
   says "top-left at (x, y)" and is **wrong**. Measured offsets from `y`, in
   units of font size `S` (verified at S=12, 24, 48): cap top `y + 0.375·S`,
   x-height top `y + 0.583·S`, **baseline `y + 1.0833·S`**, descender bottom
   `y + 1.333·S`. So `yantra_y = wantedBaselineY - 1.0833 * size`. This is
   **empirical — default typeface and weight only.** See `06-text-and-fonts.md`.
2. **A `Draw` colour must carry its alpha byte.** `Draw` primitives take
   `argb: uint32` in **AARRGGBB**. A CSS hex pasted straight in (`0xF7F9FB`) has
   alpha `0x00` and draws **fully transparent**. Node colour props are safe —
   `render/native/engine.nim:345-348` promotes a missing alpha byte to `0xFF` —
   but a `Draw` colour gets a bare `paint.setColor` (`skia_shim.cpp:1327`) with
   no rescue. So moving a colour that worked on a node into a `Draw` list makes it
   vanish. Force `0xFF` up front.
3. **`frameDt()` is the live clock; `yantra_get_dt()` is not.** `sdk/ui/input.nim:45`.
   `sdk/core/runtime.nim:75` declares `yantra_get_dt` as an importc whose only
   writer, `setFrameDt` (`host/host.nim:1211`), appears in exactly two files
   repo-wide — `render/render_qml.nim:42` (legacy Qt) and a test. An app animated
   off `yantra_get_dt()` reads 0 and never moves.
4. **`log()` is unobservable** under the headless driver, and one call measured at
   **+15,660 bytes (~7% of a ~215 KB module)**. Instrument by drawing, or by
   reading pixels.

## The three rules that save the most time

1. **Never invent an API.** Every name in this skill was read out of the SDK source. If you need something that is not in `03-node-and-prop-reference.md`, it does not exist — check `16-engine-limits-and-workarounds.md` before assuming it is missing.
2. **It compiling is not it rendering.** A prop can exist in the wire protocol and be ignored by the renderer. Always screenshot and look.
3. **Prefer a canvas for anything procedural.** An HTML page that is mostly one `<canvas>` becomes one Yantra `canvas` node and your own drawing code — not 300 nodes.

## When two chapters disagree

They will, eventually — they are a record of what was true when each was written,
not a manual. Resolve it in this order:

1. **The engine's source beats both.** Open the file (`sdk/`, `host/`, `render/`,
   `libs/nimskia/`) and read it. That is the only authority.
2. **Otherwise: later and more specific wins.**
3. **A `CORRECTED` note supersedes the text above it** in the same chapter.
4. **Measured beats documented.** The text `y` origin is exactly this case.
5. **If no rule covers it, fix the chapter** in the same change as whatever you
   were doing, with the `path:line` or the measurement that settles it.

## Repo rules that apply to you

From `AGENTS.md` — these are not suggestions:

- Never commit `third_party/`, `vendor/`, `*.exe`, `*.dll`, `*.wasm`, or anything with keys.
- Never trust a prebuilt artifact. Rebuild before claiming something works.
- Never weaken, skip or delete a test to make it pass.
- Adding a node kind or a prop = renderer mapping **plus** a `t_*.nim` test, in the same change. The SDK only promises what the renderer draws.
- Report what you measured. A reported failure is fine; a fabricated pass is not.
- Scratch work goes under `.board/`, never into the repo root.

## Keeping this skill current

This folder is a living document and **you are expected to improve it**. After you finish any port or build:

1. If you hit a gap, a workaround or a new engine fact, add it to `16-engine-limits-and-workarounds.md` (or the relevant chapter) **in the same change as the port**.
2. If you found a compiler error or a trap, add the exact error text and fix to `17-pitfalls.md`.
3. If you wrote a snippet worth reusing, add it to `examples/` **and build it** before you commit it.
4. Add a line to `CHANGELOG.md`.
5. Never document an API you did not see in the source, and never leave a `VERIFY IN SOURCE` marker behind — resolve it or delete the claim.

================================================================
FILE: skills/yantra/reference/02-language-core.md
================================================================
# 02 — The core language

Every Yantra app is a Nim file. It has no `main` requirement of its own: the SDK
gives you one shape — build a UI tree and hand it to the host — and two ways to
write it.

Both styles compile to the *same* calls on the same SDK. The declarative style is
a **compile-time rewrite** into the imperative one; there is no virtual DOM, no
diffing runtime, no second implementation. From `sdk/ui/dsl.nim:1`:

> Everything here is a compile-time rewrite into plain calls on the SDK: node
> constructors, setters, `createEffect`, and the two control-flow procs in
> ui/control.nim.

---

## 1. The two authoring styles

### 1a. Imperative / procedural

You call node constructors and hold the handles yourself. Each constructor takes
the **parent node as its first argument** and returns the new `Node`
(`sdk/ui/widgets.nim`):

```nim
proc column*(parent: Node): Node = make(nkColumn, parent)
proc row*(parent: Node): Node = make(nkRow, parent)
proc text*(parent: Node, content: string): Node =
proc button*(parent: Node, label: string): Node =
proc input*(parent: Node, hintText: string = ""): Node =
```

The whole app body goes inside `yantraRun`, which turns it into the exported
entry point the host calls (`sdk/core/runtime.nim:232`):

```nim
macro yantraRun*(body: untyped): untyped =
  result = quote do:
    proc yantra_main*() {.exportc: "yantra_main".} = `body`
```

`examples/hello.nim` is a complete app in this style — 35 lines, copied verbatim:

```nim
yantraRun:
  let win = window("Hello, Yantra", 800, 600)
  win.color(0x1e, 0x1e, 0x2e)

  let col = column(win)

  let title = text(col, "Hello, Yantra")
  title.fontSize(32)
  title.color(0xff, 0xff, 0xff)

  let subtitle = text(col, "This UI was built inside a WebAssembly sandbox.")
  subtitle.fontSize(16)
  subtitle.color(0x9a, 0x9a, 0xb0)

  var clicks = 0
  let btn = button(col, "Click me")

  btn.onClick(proc() =
    clicks += 1
    title.setText("Clicked " & $clicks & " time(s)")
    log("button clicked: " & $clicks)
  )

  let img = image(col, "logo.png")
  img.width(64)
  img.height(64)

  let nameField = input(col, "Your name")
  nameField.fontSize(14)
```

### 1b. The declarative `app(...)` macro and `{...}` blocks

You write a *tree* with indentation and named props. `examples/counter.nim`,
copied verbatim, complete:

```nim
app "Counter":
  var clicks = state 0

  column(gap = 12, pad = 24):
    text "Clicked {clicks} times", size = 32
    button "Click me":
      onClick: clicks += 1
```

Note what vanished: no parent plumbing, no handles, no `let`. The macro reads the
indentation as nesting — `column` is the parent of `text` and `button` — and
rewrites each line into the imperative call above.

### Which to prefer, and when

| | Imperative | Declarative (`app` + blocks) |
|---|---|---|
| **Prefer it for** | Logic that is really code: procs with branches, loops, timers, workers, computed graphs | Anything that is really a *view*: a screen, a card, a row, a form |
| **Reactivity** | You write `createEffect` yourself | Automatic — see §4. Any non-literal prop/text becomes an effect |
| **Handles** | You have every `Node` handle | Hidden, unless you name one with `@name` |
| **Control flow** | Manual add/remove of children | `if` / `case` / keyed `for` rebuild and dispose for you |
| **Reading a node** | `node.getStr(pValue)`, `yNodeSize(...)` | Rarely needed — signals cover it |

**Rule of thumb:** default to the declarative style for the UI, and drop to
imperative *inside a `{.component.}` proc* when the layout needs to be computed
rather than described. The two styles mix freely — a component can call
`column(win)` directly.

---

## 2. The `app(...)` macro

### Exact signature (`sdk/ui/dsl.nim:673`)

```nim
macro app*(title: string; args: varargs[untyped]): untyped =
  ## `app "Title", width = 480, height = 720:` then the UI block.
  var props: seq[NimNode] = @[]
  var body: NimNode = newStmtList()
  for a in args:
    if a.kind == nnkExprEqExpr: props.add(a)
    elif a.kind == nnkStmtList: body = a
  result = expandAppAst(title, body, props)
```

So it takes:

1. **A title string** — mandatory, first positional.
2. **`name = value` props** — any number, applied to the *window node*.
3. **One block** — the trailing `:` body.

Both spellings work; the parser distinguishes `nnkExprEqExpr` from
`nnkStmtList` automatically:

```nim
app "Counter":
  ...                                     # form 1: bare title + block

app("Todos", width = 440, height = 620, fill = 0x0B0B10):
  ...                                     # form 2: parenthesised (examples/todo.nim)
```

### What it expands to (`expandAppAst`, `dsl.nim:653`)

```nim
inner.add(newLetStmt(winSym, newCall(ident"window", title)))
for p in winProps: inner.add(buildPropSetter(winSym, "Window", p))
inner.add(transformUiBlock(body, winSym))
result = newCall(ident"yantraRun", inner)
```

Read that as: create `window(title)` → apply each window prop → transform the
block with that window node as the parent → wrap the whole thing in
`yantraRun`. So `app` is `yantraRun` + `window` + a parent-aware block, and
nothing more.

### Capabilities: `needs = ...`

One prop is not a window prop. `needs` is hoisted out of the window props and
turned into a `needs(...)` call *before* the window is created (`dsl.nim:658`):

```nim
if p.kind == nnkExprEqExpr and ($p[0]).toLowerAscii() == "needs":
  ...
  if val.kind == nnkBracket:
    var call = newCall(ident"needs")
    for elem in val: call.add elem
    inner.add call
  else:
    inner.add newCall(ident"needs", val)
```

`sdk/core/runtime.nim:35` declares the four capabilities, and the engine refuses
any call the manifest did not grant:

```nim
const
  files* = "files"
  network* = "network"
  storage* = "storage"
  notifications* = "notifications"

proc needs*(caps: varargs[string]) =
```

Usage inside an app: `app("x", needs = ["files", "network"]):` or
`needs = "files"`. `yantra_manifest_needs` is what the host reads before it even
loads the app.

### Components: `{.component.}` and `slot()`

A UI block can be lifted into an ordinary proc (`dsl.nim:682`):

```nim
macro component*(p: untyped): untyped =
  ## `proc Card(title: string) {.component.} = <UI block>`: an ordinary proc whose
  ## body is a UI block. It gets a hidden first parameter, the parent node, which
  ## the caller's UI block supplies [S-CMP-1].
```

Two hidden parameters are injected: `yParent: Node` and
`yChildren: proc (p: Node) {.closure} = nil`. At the call site
(`transformUiStmt`, `dsl.nim:557`), a capitalised name inside a UI block is a
component call, the parent is prepended, and the caller's trailing block is
passed as `yChildren`:

```nim
if name[0].isUpperAscii():
  # A component: `Card("x", 3)` -> `Card(parent, "x", 3)` [S-CMP-1]
```

Inside the component, `slot` places the caller's block (`dsl.nim:628`):

```nim
if low == "slot" and stmt.len == 1:
  # the caller's block, placed here [S-CMP-4]
  return newIfStmt((infix(ident"yChildren", "!=", newNilLit()), newCall(ident"yChildren", parentSym)))
```

Real example, `examples/todo.nim:44`:

```nim
proc TodoRow(todo: Todo) {.component.} =
  row(gap = 10, pad = 10, fill = 0x17171D, radius = 10):
    checkbox(checked = todo.done, label = todo.title, grow = 1):
      onChange: toggle(todo.id)
    button "✕", fill = 0x17171D, textColor = 0x8C8C96:
      onClick: remove(todo.id)
```

used as `TodoRow(t)` inside the loop — note it takes **no** parent argument.

### Other block-level forms

Recognised inside a UI block, all rewritten by `transformUiStmt`:

| Form | Expands to | Source |
|---|---|---|
| `hover:` / `pressed:` / `focused:` | props applied while the state holds | `dsl.nim:223` |
| `sized w, h:` | `newState(0'f32)` ×2 + `trackSize(...)` — container queries | `dsl.nim:579` |
| `scrolled y:` | `newState(0'f32)` + `trackScroll(...)` | `dsl.nim:590` |
| `lazy m in xs, 44:` | a scrolling column that builds only the visible rows | `dsl.nim:598` |
| `empty:` (after a `for`) | the empty state, disposed when the list fills | `dsl.nim:643` |
| `style(...)` / `use = …` | reusable prop bundle | `dsl.nim:710` |

`style(...)` builds a `Style` object out of non-reactive prop setters
(`dsl.nim:717`); `use = …` applies it via `applyStyle` (`sdk/ui/style.nim:54`).
Call-site props written *after* `use` win.

---

## 3. Node constructors

Two tables: the **imperative** constructors you call directly, and the
**declarative** keywords the `app` macro rewrites into those constructors (and
into `make(nk…, parent)` for the ones with no imperative twin).

### 3a. Imperative — exact signatures from source

`sdk/ui/widgets.nim` in full:

```nim
proc setText*(node: Node, value: string) = node.set(pText, value)
proc width*(node: Node, value: int32) = node.set(pWidth, value)
proc height*(node: Node, value: int32) = node.set(pHeight, value)

proc window*(title: string, w: int32 = 800, h: int32 = 600): Node =
  result = make(nkWindow); result.setText(title); result.width(w); result.height(h)

proc column*(parent: Node): Node = make(nkColumn, parent)
proc row*(parent: Node): Node = make(nkRow, parent)
proc text*(parent: Node, content: string): Node =
  result = make(nkText, parent); result.setText(content)
proc button*(parent: Node, label: string): Node =
  result = make(nkButton, parent); result.setText(label)
proc image*(parent: Node, src: string): Node =
  result = make(nkImage, parent); result.setText(src); result.set(pSource, src)
proc input*(parent: Node, hintText: string = ""): Node =
  result = make(nkInput, parent); result.setText(hintText)
proc panel*(parent: Node, w: int32 = 0, h: int32 = 0): Node =
  result = make(nkPanel, parent)
  if w > 0: result.width(w)
  if h > 0: result.height(h)
proc divider*(parent: Node, w: int32 = 0): Node {.discardable.} = panel(parent, w, 1)
proc spacer*(parent: Node, h: int32 = 8): Node {.discardable.} =
  result = make(nkSpacer, parent)
  if h > 0: result.height(h)
```

`sdk/yantra.nim` — forms, layout, media, overlays, 3D:

```nim
proc textArea*(parent: Node, placeholder: string = ""): Node     # multi-line text input
proc checkbox*(parent: Node, label: string = ""): Node            # checkbox input
proc radio*(parent: Node, group: string, label: string = ""): Node
proc select*(parent: Node, options: string = "[]"): Node          # options = JSON array
proc slider*(parent: Node, minVal: float32 = 0, maxVal: float32 = 100): Node
proc progress*(parent: Node, value: float32 = 0, maxVal: float32 = 100): Node
proc switch*(parent: Node, label: string = ""): Node
proc datePicker*(parent: Node): Node
proc colorPicker*(parent: Node): Node

proc grid*(parent: Node, cols: string = "1fr 1fr"): Node         # grid-template-columns
proc flex*(parent: Node, direction: string = "row"): Node        # row|row-reverse|column|column-reverse
proc stack*(parent: Node): Node                                   # overlay children
proc scrollArea*(parent: Node): Node
proc tabs*(parent: Node): Node
proc tabPanel*(parent: Node, label: string): Node

proc table*(parent: Node): Node
proc listView*(parent: Node): Node
proc treeView*(parent: Node): Node
proc chart*(parent: Node, chartType: string = "line"): Node       # line|bar|pie|scatter

proc video*(parent: Node, src: string = ""): Node
proc audio*(parent: Node, src: string = ""): Node
proc canvas*(parent: Node, w: int32 = 300, h: int32 = 150): Node

proc modal*(parent: Node, title: string = ""): Node
proc drawer*(parent: Node, side: string = "right"): Node          # left|right|top|bottom
proc popover*(parent: Node): Node
proc tooltip*(parent: Node, content: string): Node
proc menu*(parent: Node): Node
proc contextMenu*(parent: Node): Node

proc scene3d*(parent: Node, w: int32 = 0, h: int32 = 0): Node
proc model*(parent: Node, meshy: string = "#Cube"): Node          # param is `meshy`, not `source`
proc camera*(parent: Node): Node
proc light*(parent: Node): Node
```

The universal fallback is `make` (`sdk/core/runtime.nim:132`):

```nim
proc make*(kind: NodeKind, parent: Node = Node(id: NodeId(-1))): Node =
```

`Node` is a one-field object (`runtime.nim:9`) — `Node(id*: NodeId)` — so a
handle is just a number.

### 3b. Declarative keywords — the DSL's full `nodeKinds` list

`dsl.nim:461`, verbatim:

```nim
const nodeKinds = ["column", "row", "panel", "group", "text", "button", "input", "textarea", "checkbox", "spacer", "appview",
                   "stack", "path", "icon", "video", "page", "slide", "image", "grid", "rich", "model",
                   "modal", "drawer", "popover", "menu", "contextmenu", "tooltip",
                   "scene3d", "camera", "light", "mesh", "box", "sphere", "plane", "cylinder", "synth", "canvas", "particles"]
```

What each takes, from `nodeStmt` (`dsl.nim:293`) and the source comments:

| Keyword | Node kind | Positional arg | Purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| `column(p)` | Column | — | vertical stack of children |
| `row(p)` | Row | — | horizontal stack |
| `panel(p)` | Panel | — | a plain box |
| `group(p)` | Group | — | layout-transparent container (`control.nim:16`) |
| `text "…"` | Text | the text | one run of text; interpolated if it has `{…}` |
| `button "…"` | Button | the label | a button |
| `input(draft)` | Input | a `Signal[string]` → two-way bound | one-line text field |
| `textArea notes` | TextArea | a `Signal[string]` | many-line text field |
| `checkbox` | Checkbox | — | checkbox (`checked =`, `label =`) |
| `spacer` | Spacer | — | elastic gap; sets `pFlexGrow = 1` |
| `appview` | AppView | — | another app sandboxed in this box |
| `stack` | Stack | — | children overlap, each placed by its own top/left/… |
| `path` | Path | — | a raw path |
| `icon "search"` | Path | the icon name | an icon from the standard set; sets `pStrokeWidth = 1.8` |
| `video "clip.mp4"` | Video | the file name | video from the app's own folder |
| `page` | Page | — | one page of a PDF |
| `slide` | Page | — | a 1280×720 deck slide (width/height preset) |
| `image "logo.png"` | Image | the source path | a picture |
| `grid "1fr 2fr 200"` | Grid | the columns string | px / `fr` / `auto` columns; `span = 2` |
| `rich "…**bold**…"` | Text | the markup | inline styled text: `**bold**`, `[a link](url)` |
| `model "fox.glb"` | Model | the file name | a glTF 2.0 model, lit and framed |
| `modal` / `drawer` / `popover` / `menu` / `contextmenu` | Modal… | — | overlays above the window, not in layout |
| `tooltip "Copy link"` | Tooltip | the text | shown after the pointer rests on the node |
| `scene3d` / `camera` / `light` | Scene3D… | — | native 3D (Filament) |
| `box`/`sphere`/`plane`/`cylinder`/`mesh` | Mesh | — | 3D objects inside a `scene3d` |
| `synth` | Synth | — | a sound the engine makes; not laid out |
| `canvas` | Canvas | — | 2D drawing (paint.nim's `Draw`) |
| `particles` | Particles | — | sprites the engine emits and moves |

Notes straight from the source comments:

- Overlays: *"a modal is centred over a dimmed window, a drawer docks to an edge,
  a popover or menu opens by the node it is inside, a context menu at a right click
  on that node. `open = sig` shows it (absent: open while it exists); Escape or a
  click outside fires `onDismiss`"* (`dsl.nim:311`).
- 3D: *"`scene3d(sky = 0x87CEEB, fog = 0.02):` is a view into a 3D world; inside
  it `box`/`sphere`/`plane`/`cylinder`/`mesh(geometry = buf)` are objects
  (posX.., rotY.., sizeX.., fill, metal, rough, glow, instances = buf), `light`
  and `camera` light and see it, and `model "fox.glb"` is an object too. Filament
  draws it; the objects are not laid out."* (`dsl.nim:338`)
- Grid: *"`grid "1fr 2fr 200", spacing = 12:` — columns of fixed px, shares (`fr`)
  or `auto`; children fill them in order, row after row (`span = 2` takes two
  columns)"* (`dsl.nim:419`).

Two important DSL-only behaviours:

**Interpolation** (`dsl.nim:262`, `textLike`) — a literal text argument is set
once; a text with `{…}` in it becomes an effect that rewrites itself:

```nim
if mainArg.kind in {nnkStrLit, nnkTripleStrLit}:
  let (exprAst, isReactive) = parseInterpolatedExpr(mainArg.strVal, mainArg)
  if isReactive:
    result.add(newLetStmt(nodeSym, newCall(ident(ctor), parentSym, newLit(""))))
    result.add(effect(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"setText"), exprAst)))
```

A non-literal first argument (e.g. a signal) is `$`-wrapped inside an effect.

**Named handles** — `takeHandle` (`dsl.nim:276`) pulls `text @headline "Hi"` or
`panel(@card, …)` out of the statement and sets it as `pName`, *"a stable name
for code, tests, tools and AI edits"*.

Every node also gets `pSite` set to its source position in dev builds
(`dsl.nim:448`), so hot reload and inspectors can jump from a pixel to its line.

### 3c. Imperative-only constructors

Not in `nodeKinds` (so not available as DSL keywords, though `divider` is
reachable as `panel(parent, w, 1)`):

- `divider(parent, w = 0)`
- `table`, `listView`, `treeView`, `chart`, `tabs`, `tabPanel`, `flex`
- `scrollArea`, `audio`, `radio`, `slider`, `progress`, `switch`, `datePicker`,
  `colorPicker`

These are **not** usable as UI-block keywords. `transformUiStmt` checks
`low in nodeKinds` first, then falls through to
`else: error("unknown node kind " & name)` (`dsl.nim:440`). And a capitalised
name is treated as a *component* call, which prepends `yParent` — so writing
`Radio(p, "g")` in a block would expand to `Radio(yParent, p, "g")`, not what
you meant.

A `{.component.}` proc body is *also* a UI block, so the same rule applies
there. To reach these kinds from inside one, use the escape hatch the DSL itself
uses — `make` with the kind enum:

```nim
make(nkCheckbox, yParent)
```

> **RESOLVED 2026-10-03.** This used to carry an open verification marker asking
> you to confirm the spelling. Confirmed: the enum is
> `NodeKind* {.size: sizeof(int32).} = enum` at `sdk/core/wire.nim:5`, and every
> kind is `nk<Name>` — `nkWindow = 0` (`sdk/core/wire.nim:6`) through
> `nkParticles = 47` (`sdk/core/wire.nim:53`). That file is **generated**
> (`sdk/core/wire.nim:1-2`: "GENERATED FILE. DO NOT EDIT. Edit
> `protocol/schema.nim` instead"), so the spellings come from
> `protocol/schema.nim`. `03-node-and-prop-reference.md` is the table to read;
> the source of truth for the spelling is `sdk/core/wire.nim`.

---

## 4. State & reactivity

`sdk/core/signals.nim` header, verbatim:

> Three ideas, as in docs/spec/core.md §5:
> * a `Signal` holds a value and knows which effects read it;
> * an `Effect` re-runs when a signal it read in its latest run changes. Before
>   every run it forgets its old dependencies and disposes what it created, so it
>   only ever depends on what it actually read last time [S-RX-5];
> * an `Owner` is a scope. Effects, child scopes and clean-ups belong to the
>   scope that was current when they were created.

### The type

```nim
type
  Signal*[T] = ref object of SignalBase
    value: T
```

### Creating — `state`

The template you use in apps (`signals.nim:198`):

```nim
template state*(initial: untyped): untyped =
  ## A signal holding `initial`. It keeps its value when the app is hot-reloaded.
  let s = newState(initial)
  when not defined(release):
    keepAcrossReloads(s, instantiationInfo(-1).filename, instantiationInfo(-1).line)
  s
```

and the raw constructor behind it (`signals.nim:181`):

```nim
proc newState*[T](initial: T): Signal[T] =
  Signal[T](value: initial, subscribers: newSeqOfCap[Effect](4))
```

Real usage, `examples/counter.nim` and `examples/todo.nim`:

```nim
var clicks = state 0
var todos = state(@[Todo(id: 1, title: "Write the pitch"), Todo(id: 2, title: "Record the demo")])
var draft = state("")
var filter = state("All")
```

`state` is a `varargs`-free template, so it works with any expression.

### Reading and writing

```nim
proc get*[T](s: Signal[T]): lent T =
  ## Reads and subscribes. Borrowed, not copied: reading a list of 100 000 items costs
  ## nothing (copy it with `var x = s.get` to change it).
  track(s)
  s.value

proc peek*[T](s: Signal[T]): lent T = s.value        ## reads WITHOUT subscribing

proc set*[T](s: Signal[T]; v: T) =
  if s.value == v: return
  s.value = v
  schedule(s.subscribers)

proc update*[T](s: Signal[T]; fn: proc(v: var T)) =
  ## Mutates in place and always notifies [S-RX-3].
  fn(s.value)
  schedule(s.subscribers)

proc `+=`*[T](s: Signal[T]; delta: T) = s.set(s.value + delta)
proc `-=`*[T](s: Signal[T]; delta: T) = s.set(s.value - delta)
```

The full write vocabulary:

```nim
clicks += 1          # the += operator
todos.update(proc(xs: var seq[Todo]) = xs.add Todo(id: nextId, title: title))
draft.set("")
filter.set(name)
```

Note `set` short-circuits on an unchanged value; `update` **always** notifies,
which is what you want for in-place mutation of a collection.

### The implicit unwrap (converter)

```nim
converter val*[T](s: Signal[T]): T =
  ## Implicit unwrap of a Signal[T] to its inner value T while tracking subscriptions [S-RX-1].
  s.get()
```

This is why `text "Clicked {clicks} times"` works without `.get`. Also:

```nim
proc `$`*[T](s: Signal[T]): string = $(s.get())
```

### `createEffect`

```nim
proc createEffect*(fn: proc()): Effect =
  ## Runs `fn` now and again whenever a signal it read changes. Belongs to the
  ## current scope, so disposing that scope stops it.
```

It runs **immediately**, then again on any change. Imperative example, from
`skills/yantra/examples/02-imperative.nim`:

```nim
let label = text(page, "Clicks: 0")
label.fontSize(28)

createEffect:
  label.setText("Clicks: " & $(count.get))

let add = button(row, "Click me")
add.width(120)
add.onClick(proc() = batch(proc() = count.set(count.get + 1)))
```

**You never write `createEffect` in the declarative style.** The macro emits one
for every non-literal prop or text (`dsl.nim:64`):

```nim
proc effect(stmt: NimNode): NimNode =
  newTree(nnkDiscardStmt, newCall(ident"createEffect", lambda(newStmtList(stmt))))

proc reactiveOrOnce(stmt: NimNode; value: NimNode): NimNode =
  if isLiteral(value): stmt else: effect(stmt)
```

`isLiteral` (`dsl.nim:52`) is the whole heuristic — int, float, char, string,
triple-string literals, plus `true`/`false`/`nil`, plus any identifier that looks
like a constant (`al…` with an uppercase third char):

```nim
proc isLiteral(n: NimNode): bool =
  case n.kind
  of nnkCharLit .. nnkFloat128Lit, nnkStrLit .. nnkTripleStrLit: true
  of nnkIdent, nnkSym: ($n) in ["true", "false", "nil"] or ($n).startsWith("al") and ($n).len > 2 and ($n)[2].isUpperAscii
  of nnkPrefix: n.len == 2 and isLiteral(n[1])
  else: false
```

`examples/todo.nim:52` shows the derived case — a prop whose value is a whole
`if` expression becomes one effect that re-runs when `filter` changes:

```nim
proc FilterTab(name: string) {.component.} =
  button name, fill = (if filter.get() == name: 0xFFB23E else: 0x17171D),
         textColor = (if filter.get() == name: 0x1A1206 else: 0xC9C9D2):
    onClick: filter.set(name)
```

### `batch`

```nim
proc batch*(fn: proc()) =
  inc batchDepth
  try:
    fn()
  finally:
    dec batchDepth
  if batchDepth == 0 and not flushing:
    flush()
```

Writes inside one `batch` produce exactly **one** re-render, not one per write.
The DSL applies it for free — every event handler is wrapped (`dsl.nim:253`):

```nim
proc eventHandler(nodeSym: NimNode; ev: string; body: NimNode): NimNode =
  ## node.on(evX, proc() = batch(proc() = body))
  let batched = newCall(ident"batch", lambda(body))
  newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"on"), ident(ev), lambda(newStmtList(batched)))
```

Imperatively you must remember it yourself (see the `onClick` above).

### Derived signals

```nim
proc derived*[T](fn: proc(): T): Signal[T] =
  ## A value computed from other signals and kept up to date [S-RX-5].
  let s = Signal[T](subscribers: newSeqOfCap[Effect](4))
  discard createEffect(proc() = s.set(fn()))
  s

template derive*(body: untyped): untyped =
  ## `let total = derive: items.get.len * price.get` — `derived` without the proc (S11).
  derived(proc (): auto = body)
```

### Scopes and cleanup

```nim
proc newOwner*(parent: Owner = currentOwner): Owner
proc getOwner*(): Owner = currentOwner
proc disposeOwner*(o: Owner): Owner          ## children, then effects, then clean-ups, each in reverse creation order. Idempotent.
proc runWithOwner*(o: Owner; fn: proc())     ## runs fn with o as the current scope, outside any effect and untracked
proc onCleanup*(fn: proc())                  ## inside an effect: runs before its next run; otherwise: when the current scope is disposed
proc untrack*[T](fn: proc(): T): T          ## reads inside subscribe nothing
```

### Interpolation — `"{n} times"`

`parseInterpolatedExpr` (`dsl.nim:12`), the doc comment is the spec:

```nim
proc parseInterpolatedExpr*(s: string; info: NimNode): (NimNode, bool) =
  ## "Clicked {n} times" -> `"Clicked " & $(n) & " times"`, and whether it had any `{…}`.
```

It splits on `{…}`, parses each inner expression with `parseExpr`, wraps it in
`$`, and joins with `&`. It only goes reactive if something was actually
interpolated (`hasInterp`). Real examples, from the examples folder:

```nim
text "Clicked {clicks} times", size = 32                              # counter.nim:5
text "{left()} left of {todos.get().len}", size = 14, color = 0x8C8C96 # todo.nim:59
```

Note the second: an arbitrary call is fine inside `{…}`, so computed values read
their own signals and stay current.

**Limits.** The interpolation parser is hand-rolled over the *string*, not the
AST, so anything that contains `}` inside the braces confuses it, and nested
braces are not supported. `interpolateStr` (`dsl.nim:44`) is the runtime twin,
replacing `{name}` from an `openArray[(string, string)]`.

### Reactive `if` / `case`

Both expand to `showWhen` (`dsl.nim:491`, `dsl.nim:511`):

```nim
proc showWhen*(parent: Node; branchOf: proc(): int; builds: seq[proc(parent: Node)]) =
  ## `if`/`elif`/`else`: `branchOf` returns the index of the branch to show
  ## (-1 = none). Only a change of branch rebuilds; the branch's own effects keep
  ## it up to date otherwise [S-CTL-1].
```

The branch selector is a real `if` expression whose result is the branch index,
so the conditions are ordinary Nim. `examples/todo.nim:74`:

```nim
if todos.get().len > left():
  button "Clear done", fill = 0x0B0B10, textColor = 0x8C8C96:
    onClick: clearDone()
```

**Every branch lives in its own `Owner` scope inside a `Group`** (`control.nim:21`):

```nim
proc buildScoped(outer: Owner; parent: Node; build: proc(parent: Node)): (Owner, Node) =
  ## Builds `build` into a fresh Group under `parent`, inside a fresh scope under
  ## `outer`. Disposing the scope removes the Group and everything in it.
```

So switching branches disposes the old scope: its effects stop and its nodes are
removed from the engine, *with everything below them*. That is the mechanism
behind "no leaks in a loop".

`case` works identically and gets its own transform (`dsl.nim:511`).

### Keyed `for`

```nim
proc keyedList*[T](parent: Node; source: proc(): seq[T]; keyOf: proc(x: T): string;
                   build: proc(parent: Node; item: T); empty: proc(parent: Node) = nil) =
  ## Keyed `for`: one scope per key [S-CTL-2, S-NODE-2].
  ##   * a key that stays with the same value keeps its nodes and state untouched;
  ##   * a key whose value changed is rebuilt in place (same position);
  ##   * a new key is built; a key that goes is disposed, removing its nodes;
  ##   * a move reorders the existing nodes instead of rebuilding them.
  ## Duplicate keys keep only the first occurrence [S-CTL-3]. `empty` is shown
  ## while the list is empty (the `empty:` block after the loop) [S-CTL-5].
```

**Syntax** (`forStmt`, `dsl.nim:466`): one loop variable, optional `.keyed(...)`:

```nim
for x in items:              # the item itself is the key
for x in items.keyed(x.id):  # key by an id
```

An `empty:` block immediately after the loop is consumed as the empty state
(`dsl.nim:643`). Real example, `examples/todo.nim:70`:

```nim
column(gap = 8):
  for t in shown().keyed(t.id):
    TodoRow(t)
  empty:
    text "Nothing here yet.", color = 0x55555E
```

`shown()` is an ordinary proc reading signals (`todo.nim:36`) — that is what
makes the loop source reactive: `keyedList` reads it inside `createEffect`.

What iterates (`control.nim:120`):

```nim
proc listValue*[T](s: Signal[seq[T]]): seq[T] = s.get()   ## a list signal is read (and so tracked)...
proc listValue*[T](s: seq[T]): seq[T] = s                  ## ...a plain list is used as it is.
proc listValue*[I, T](a: array[I, T]): seq[T] = @a        ## ...and a fixed array becomes a list.
```

### Bound inputs

`input draft` is a **two-way** binding to a `Signal[string]` (`dsl.nim:372`):

```nim
# `input draft`: two-way binding to a Signal[string] [S-CMP-3]
let sig = stmt[1]
result.add(effect(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"set"), ident"pValue",
                          newCall(ident"get", sig))))
result.add(newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"on"), ident"evInput",
  lambda(newStmtList(newCall(ident"set", sig,
    newCall(newDotExpr(nodeSym, ident"getStr"), ident"pValue"))))))
```

Real usage, `examples/todo.nim:61`:

```nim
input(draft, placeholder = "What needs doing?", grow = 1, fill = 0x17171D, radius = 10):
  onSubmit: add()
```

Signal → widget in an effect; widget → signal on `evInput`. The `onSubmit:`
coexists — `addHandler` keeps a list per (node, event), so *"an `input` bound to
a signal can also have its own `onInput:`"* (`runtime.nim:176`).

`textArea notes` binds the same way. `checkbox` is **not** auto-bound — it takes
`checked =` as a prop and you wire `onChange:` yourself (`todo.nim:46`).

### Container queries — signals fed by the engine

Two more signal sources, both imperative (`sdk/yantra.nim`):

```nim
proc trackSize*(node: Node; w, h: Signal[float32])
  ## Keeps `w` and `h` at the node's laid-out size (px): the engine sends Resize when it
  ## changes, before painting. `sized w, h:` in the DSL uses it [S-LAY-2].
proc trackScroll*(node: Node; y: Signal[float32])
  ## Keeps `y` at the node's scroll offset (px): the engine sends Scroll when it changes.
proc trackMedia*(node: Node; time, duration: Signal[float32])
```

In the DSL these become `sized w, h:` and `scrolled y:` (`dsl.nim:579`, `590`),
which allocate the signals for you:

```nim
sized w, h:
  if w.get() < 640: ...
```

---

## 5. Events

### The declarative table — all 16, verbatim

`dsl.nim:70`:

```nim
const eventNames = [("onclick", "evClick"), ("onchange", "evChange"), ("oninput", "evInput"),
                    ("onsubmit", "evSubmit"), ("onfocus", "evFocus"), ("onblur", "evBlur"),
                    ("ondismiss", "evDismiss"), ("oncontextmenu", "evContextMenu"), ("onkey", "evKey"),
                    ("ondragstart", "evDragStart"), ("ondragover", "evDragOver"), ("ondrop", "evDrop"),
                    ("onframe", "evFrame"), ("onpointerdown", "evPointerDown"),
                    ("onpointermove", "evPointerMove"), ("onpointerup", "evPointerUp")]
```

| Block keyword | Event enum | Wire id (`wire.nim`) |
|---|---|---|
| `onClick:` | `evClick` | 0 |
| `onChange:` | `evChange` | 1 |
| `onInput:` | `evInput` | 3 |
| `onSubmit:` | `evSubmit` | 2 |
| `onFocus:` | `evFocus` | 4 |
| `onBlur:` | `evBlur` | 5 |
| `onDismiss:` | `evDismiss` | 14 |
| `onContextMenu:` | `evContextMenu` | 13 |
| `onKey:` | `evKey` | 15 |
| `onDragStart:` | `evDragStart` | 30 |
| `onDragOver:` | `evDragOver` | 31 |
| `onDrop:` | `evDrop` | 32 |
| `onFrame:` | `evFrame` | 33 |
| `onPointerDown:` | `evPointerDown` | 20 |
| `onPointerMove:` | `evPointerMove` | 21 |
| `onPointerUp:` | `evPointerUp` | 22 |

**Case is irrelevant.** Lookup lowercases the source name
(`dsl.nim:78`):

```nim
proc eventOf(name: string): string =
  for (k, v) in eventNames:
    if k == name.toLowerAscii(): return v
  ""
```

So `onClick:`, `onclick:` and `ONCLICK:` are the same block.

**Syntax.** It is a *block*, not a lambda — `onClick: clicks += 1`, with no
`proc()` and no `=`. The body becomes `node.on(evX, proc() = batch(proc() = body))`.

**An event attaches to the node it is written inside.** In
`examples/todo.nim:46-49`, the `onChange:` belongs to the `checkbox`, the
`onClick:` to the button — the child block, not the enclosing `row`.

### The full Event enum

`Event* {.size: sizeof(int32).} = enum` (`wire.nim:322`), all 34 values:

```nim
evClick = 0        evChange = 1       evSubmit = 2        evInput = 3
evFocus = 4        evBlur = 5         evScroll = 6        evTabChange = 7
evSelectionChange = 8                 evMediaEnd = 9      evMediaError = 10
evCanvasDraw = 11  evResize = 12      evContextMenu = 13  evDismiss = 14
evKey = 15         evTimeUpdate = 16  evMediaReady = 17   evBlockSplit = 18
evBlockMerge = 19  evPointerDown = 20 evPointerMove = 21  evPointerUp = 22
evLinkClick = 23   evCommand = 24     evMenuCommand = 25  evSystemTheme = 26
evDeepLink = 27    evSpellMenu = 28   evCallSignal = 29   evDragStart = 30
evDragOver = 31    evDrop = 32        evFrame = 33
```

The 18 without a block keyword are reachable imperatively via `on(node, ev, fn)`
or a named helper — see below.

### Imperative handlers — exact signatures

The generic form (`sdk/yantra.nim:384`):

```nim
proc on*(node: Node, event: Event, handler: proc()) =
  node.addHandler(event, handler)
```

`sdk/core/runtime.nim`, exact:

```nim
proc addHandler*(node: Node; event: Event; handler: proc())
proc clickable*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.addHandler(evClick, handler)
proc onClick*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.addHandler(evClick, handler)
proc onChange*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.addHandler(evChange, handler)
proc onDragStart*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.addHandler(evDragStart, handler)
proc onDragOver*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.addHandler(evDragOver, handler)
proc onDrop*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.addHandler(evDrop, handler)
```

`sdk/yantra.nim`, exact:

```nim
proc onInput*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evInput, handler)
proc onFocus*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evFocus, handler)
proc onBlur*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evBlur, handler)
proc onScroll*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evScroll, handler)
proc onTabChange*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evTabChange, handler)
proc onSelectionChange*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evSelectionChange, handler)
proc onMediaEnd*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evMediaEnd, handler)
proc onMediaError*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evMediaError, handler)
proc onCanvasDraw*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evCanvasDraw, handler)
proc onMediaReady*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evMediaReady, handler)
proc onTimeUpdate*(node: Node, handler: proc()) = node.on(evTimeUpdate, handler)
```

`onDrop` is **overloaded** — the `runtime.nim` one takes `proc()`, the
`runtime.nim:222` one takes the dropped file:

```nim
type DroppedFile* = object
  name*: string
  data*: string

proc droppedFile*(): DroppedFile = DroppedFile(name: droppedName(), data: droppedData())
proc onDrop*(node: Node, handler: proc(f: DroppedFile)) =
  node.addHandler(evDrop, proc() = handler(droppedFile()))
```

Plus the drag source and the editor/OS helpers:

```nim
proc setDragData*(data: string)                 # runtime.nim:201
proc spellMenu*(): (string, seq[string])       # runtime.nim:308 — misspelling + suggestions
proc spellRange*(node: Node): (int, int)       # runtime.nim:316
proc menuCommand*(): int32                     # runtime.nim:297 — which item, in a MenuCommand handler
proc systemDark*(): bool                       # runtime.nim:298
proc accentRgb*(): int32                       # runtime.nim:299 — 0xRRGGBB
proc deepLinkUrl*(): string                    # runtime.nim:300 — in a DeepLink handler
proc mediaTime*(node: Node): float32           # yantra.nim:418
proc mediaDuration*(node: Node): float32       # yantra.nim:420
proc waveform*(node: Node): seq[float32]       # yantra.nim:429
```

### Multiple handlers, and the input reading idiom

```nim
proc addHandler*(node: Node; event: Event; handler: proc()) =
  ## Any number of handlers per node and event, run in the order they were added
  ## (an `input` bound to a signal can also have its own `onInput:`). The engine
  ## sees one handler per (node, event): a dispatcher over this list.
```

To read what the user typed (`runtime.nim:72`):

```nim
proc yCapture*(kind, op, name, nameLen: int32): int32 {.importc: "y_capture", cdecl.}
  ## Copies up to `cap` bytes of a string property into the app's memory at `buf`;
  ## returns the full length. How an app reads what the user typed.
```

and the public wrappers:

```nim
proc getStr*(node: Node, prop: Prop): string
```

```nim
btn.onClick(proc() =
  let q = input.getStr(pValue)
  log("user typed: " & q))
```

The input can also be edited and replaced as an undoable change
(`runtime.nim:304`):

```nim
proc replaceText*(node: Node; a, z: int; text: string) =
  ## Replaces bytes a ..< z of an input's value as an edit (undoable; the field fires Input).
```

---

## 6. Imperative setters

Every setter takes the `Node` first. The universal one is `set`, with four
overloads by value type (`sdk/core/runtime.nim:138-170`):

```nim
proc set*(node: Node, prop: Prop, value: string)
proc set*(node: Node, prop: Prop, value: float32)
proc set*(node: Node, prop: Prop, value: int32)
proc set*(node: Node, prop: Prop, value: bool)
```

Everything else is a thin, named wrapper over it.

### Box model & geometry

`sdk/yantra.nim`, exact signatures with their real doc comments:

```nim
proc fontSize*(node: Node, value: int32) = node.set(pFontSize, value)

proc spacing*(node: Node, value: int32) =
  ## Gap between the children of a Column or Row. Previously this was hardcoded
  ## to 10 in the renderer, so every gap on a page was identical.
  node.set(pSpacing, value)

proc padding*(node: Node, all: int32)          ## Uniform inset, on any side. Sets all four.
proc padding*(node: Node, top, right, bottom, left: int32)

proc radius*(node: Node, value: int32)         ## Corner radius, in pixels.

proc border*(node: Node, width: int32, r, g, b: int32) =
  ## Border width plus its colour, in one call so a border is never half-set.
```

`padding` is genuinely overloaded — one arg for all four sides, four for
per-side.

### Appearance

```nim
proc color*(node: Node, r, g, b: int32) =
  ## 0xRRGGBB packed, so a colour is one integer across the boundary. For a
  ## Model this is the material's base colour.
  node.set(pColor, (r shl 16) or (g shl 8) or b)

proc x*(node: Node, value: float32) = node.set(pX, value)
proc y*(node: Node, value: float32) = node.set(pY, value)
proc visible*(node: Node, value: bool) = node.set(pVisible, value)
proc opacity*(node: Node, value: float32) = node.set(pOpacity, value)

type
  Align* {.size: sizeof(int32).} = enum
    alLeft   = 0
    alCenter = 1
    alRight  = 2

proc align*(node: Node, a: Align) =
  ## Text alignment. Was a dead property until the renderer started emitting it.
  node.set(pAlign, int32(a))
```

Size, from `sdk/ui/widgets.nim`:

```nim
proc width*(node: Node, value: int32) = node.set(pWidth, value)
proc height*(node: Node, value: int32) = node.set(pHeight, value)
proc setText*(node: Node, value: string) = node.set(pText, value)
```

Note `color` takes **three bytes**, while in the DSL you write one packed int
(`fill = 0x17171D`). `examples/hello.nim:15` uses `title.color(0xff, 0xff, 0xff)`;
`examples/todo.nim:45` uses `fill = 0x17171D`. Both spellings are correct for
their style.

### Style values — colours with alpha, gradients, shadows

`sdk/ui/style.nim`:

```nim
type
  Gradient* = object
    ## `fill = linear(0xFF7A2F, 0xFFD28A, 135)`: from, to, angle in degrees (90 = top to bottom).
    a*, b*: int
    angle*: float32
  Shadow* = object
    ## `shadow = shadow(y = 8, blur = 24, color = rgba(0x000000, 0.4))`
    x*, y*, blur*: float32
    color*: int
  Style* = object
    apply*: seq[proc (n: Node)]

proc rgba*(rgb: int; alpha: float): int
proc linear*(a, b: int; angle = 90.0): Gradient = Gradient(a: a, b: b, angle: float32(angle))
proc shadow*(x = 0.0; y = 6.0; blur = 18.0; color = rgba(0x000000, 0.35)): Shadow
proc setFill*(n: Node; c: int) = …          ## also switches any gradient off (pGradAngle = -1)
proc setFill*(n: Node; g: Gradient) = …
proc setShadow*(n: Node; s: Shadow) = …
proc applyStyle*(n: Node; s: Style) = for f in s.apply: f(n)
```

`rgba`'s alpha note is worth internalising: *"Alpha 0 is kept as 'almost clear'
so it stays distinguishable from a plain 0xRRGGBB (which means opaque)."*

### Forms & inputs

```nim
proc value*(node: Node, v: string)  = node.set(pValue, v)
proc value*(node: Node, v: float32) = node.set(pValue, v)
proc value*(node: Node, v: int32)   = node.set(pValue, v)
proc value*(node: Node, v: bool)    = node.set(pValue, v)

proc placeholder*(node: Node, v: string)
proc min*(node: Node, v: float32)   proc max*(node: Node, v: float32)
proc step*(node: Node, v: float32)
proc checked*(node: Node, v: bool)
proc options*(node: Node, v: string)
proc multiple*(node: Node, v: bool)  proc disabled*(node: Node, v: bool)
proc required*(node: Node, v: bool)  proc readOnly*(node: Node, v: bool)
proc maxLength*(node: Node, v: int32) proc pattern*(node: Node, v: string)
```

### Layout

```nim
proc flexGrow*(node: Node, v: float32)   proc flexShrink*(node: Node, v: float32)
proc flexBasis*(node: Node, v: float32)
proc gridCols*(node: Node, v: string)    proc gridRows*(node: Node, v: string)
proc gridGap*(node: Node, v: int32)      proc zIndex*(node: Node, v: int32)
proc overflow*(node: Node, v: string)    proc tabIndex*(node: Node, v: int32)
proc scrollX*(node: Node, v: int32)      proc scrollY*(node: Node, v: int32)
```

### Tabs, tables, media, canvas, overlays

```nim
proc columns*(node: Node, v: string)   proc rows*(node: Node, v: string)
proc selection*(node: Node, v: string) proc sortable*(node: Node, v: bool)
proc filterable*(node: Node, v: bool)

proc src*(node: Node, v: string)       proc poster*(node: Node, v: string)
proc autoplay*(node: Node, v: bool)    proc loop*(node: Node, v: bool)
proc muted*(node: Node, v: bool)       proc volume*(node: Node, v: float32)
proc playbackRate*(node: Node, v: float32)

proc canvasSize*(node: Node, w, h: int32)

proc open*(node: Node, v: bool)                    ## pModalOpen — shows/hides an overlay
proc title*(node: Node, v: string)                 ## pModalTitle
proc closeOnEsc*(node: Node, v: bool)
proc closeOnClickOutside*(node: Node, v: bool)
```

### 3D setters

```nim
proc source*(node: Node, value: string)   ## "#Cube" or an asset path, for a Model
proc scale*(node: Node, value: float32)   ## uniform — one number, not three
proc rotation*(node: Node, rx, ry, rz: float32)   ## Euler degrees
proc position*(node: Node, px, py, pz: float32)
```
